![](https://i.ormos.uk/de/b8/deb841043692ff9303d681f7d4351ad8596e788aa147614fe418daa76d8dfd15.jpg)

Just as Rodney Harrison plucked a first-quarter pass to stop a Philadelphia touchdown, Masashi Oiso pulled an amazing win out of what looked like a imminent defeat. With Lucas Glavin's deck promising a huge [Sutured Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sutured+Ghoul), the theatrical Japanese walked the huge American into a trap.

It was a dramatic end to a Extended Grand Prix that did little to help solve the riddle of the Qualifying season. It's doubtful anyone left this room with any decent idea of what to play, and what to expect from events this season. There are a lot of cool new decks -- check Glavin's, Dempsey's or McLaughlin's just in the Top 8 -- but no clear "deck to beat."

The Grand Prix was clearly marked by that and the Japanese Road Trip. Are the big names in Asia about to flex their muscle all over the world? Or did they just come to see the Super Bowl? Congratulations to Masashi Oiso for his brilliant win and please don't be too harsh on Lucas Glavin. Playing in a premier event Top 8 is never easy. Just ask Brian "No Blocks" Davis.

**Congratulations to Masashi Oiso -- the 2005 Pro Tour Boston champion!**

## BLOG

  
![](https://i.ormos.uk/a4/80/a4805ad1ab91968e067166b22bc86f14a4fc13663da64409b636a3b37dde918d.jpg)*Scott M. Fischer has created some of Magic's most stunning.*

The artist on site today is a veteran of premier event signing. I clearly remember him doing his trademark "fish" signature in Europe more than once. He has a few cards in the Kamigawa block ([Time Stop](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Stop) and [Meloku the Clouded Mirror](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meloku+the+Clouded+Mirror)) so players should have some news one to sign.

Here's a quick list of some of Fischer's cards you might have come accross in Constructed matches.

## Saturday, Feb. 5: 3:34 pm - The Three Bye Metagame

![](https://i.ormos.uk/85/61/8561e8b57364ff32891a8c54218927781c2b699e86d799c1eb8b86e20a649cf8.jpg)*That's a lot of decklists.*

While most players are out there slugging it out on the third round, sixty lucky players are kicking it back at the hotel or in the side events area. Their three free wins will sure come in handy on a nine-round day like today. So I took a deep breath and went through the huge decklist folder looking for their decklists.

Here's the the archetype breakdown for those players, apart from three lists that I couldn't seem to find. I clumped together some decks that might be a bit different (let's say Goblins with Black and straight-Red Goblins or Scepter-Chant and Scepter-Chant with Black).

Affinity: 3  
 Aluren: 2  
 Black-White: 1  
 Scepter-Chant: 2  
 Confinement: 2  
 Desire: 10  
 Dump Truck: 1  
 Elves: 1  
 Enchantress: 3  
 Goblins: 6  
 Life: 4  
 RDW: 9  
 Reanimator: 3  
 Rock: 5  
 UG: 3  
 WWu: 1  
 Welder-Reanimator: 1

More on that Welder-Reanimator deck later.

  

## Saturday, Feb. 5: 9:23 pm - Gabe Walls, the Matchmaker

![](https://i.ormos.uk/59/56/595603fccb896d689c8a1690b44f281dad737f821ec95a94af046846057b0bd3.jpg)*Gabe Walls and his creation.*

Probably the most unusual decklist I saw so far certainly belongs to Gabe Walls. The veteran Pro Tour player took a page from a Columbus PTQ and brought mix of Welder.dec and... Reanimator.dec. First he uses [Careful Study](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Careful+Study) and [Intuition](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Intuition) to get the a huge monster into his graveyard and then [Reanimate](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reanimate), [Goblin Welder](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Welder) or [Exhume](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exhume) bring in them to the playing area.

This plan allows him to bring in [Sundering Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sundering+Titan)[Platinum Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Platinum+Angel) or [Bosh, Iron Golem](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bosh%2C+Iron+Golem). With the Welder he can keep things going... and if he starts recurring [Mindslaver](https://web.archive.org/web/20220128082422/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mindslaver)... you probably just want to scoop and save yourself any further beatings.
